[[Mardin]] [[Lug]] [[Bingöl]] [[Mwah.]] [[Adana]] 6) Ayla Akat Ata [[Batman]] [[Şırnak]] [[Bitlis]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] [[Hakkari]] [[Hakkari]] 14) The Leader of the Secret [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[Kars]] [[Mersin]] [[Mwah.]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Van]] [[Van]] Hundreds of prisoners and convicts are currently in prison in Turkey, including death fasts, continuing their hunger strike to protest obstacles to an equal life. The hunger strikes, which began on September 12, 2012, on the anniversary of the 1980 coup, continue to increase, not only because of the government's irresponsible and insensitive policies, but in a new prison every day, including children. Hunger strikes are a political action that prisoners resort to as a last resort. Prisoners currently on hunger strike have two reasonable and understandable demands. One is the removal of all obstacles in front of the main language, including education and defence in the basic universal human rights of each human, and one is the opening of dialogue and negotiation routes to end the conflicting process that has dominated our country for years. The way is through the launch of talks with PKK leader Abdullah Ocalan, who is agreed to be the one to end the conflicting process, which everyone has accepted in some way. Abdullah Ocalan has not been interviewed for months, including his lawyers, but has been deprived of his legal rights. The activists who were on hunger strike did not resort to this act to correct prison conditions, but to the good of the country and to allow the people to live as brothers. Education and defence in the native language are sacred. There's nothing more terrible than a person not being able to defend himself in the native language. At the moment, in some judicial cases in our country, the defendants may be able to defend themselves in their native language, translate what they say through the interpreter. However, as for political cases, Kurdish is listed as an unknown language. No one should be forced to defend himself in a language he does not control. A person should be able to defend himself in his best language. The court does not have the right to make a decision that can only be made by the person himself. The freedom of the individual at stake is one of the main duties of democratic law states to give him the opportunity to defend himself. The demands of activists on hunger strike are demands already in the law. "Everyone has the right to a fair trial as a prosecutor or a defendant in front of the judicial authorities by using the means and means of the right to seek the rights of our Constitution." he says. In this case, the defense is a constitutional right. The third article of the Turkish Penal Code also states that "in implementing the Penal Code there is no distinction between race, language, religion, religion, nationality, color, gender, political or other ideas, philosophical belief, national or social origin, birth, economic and other social positions, and no one is allowed to be privileged." As such, the right to the defence, which is under constitutional guarantee, is also guaranteed by the Penal Code, and is eventually translated into the basis of the case and defence by the court's interpreter, if the accused or the victim does not know enough Turkish to express his identity." The main framework and measures needed to be taken for the defence's actual fulfillment. Hunger strikes have entered a critical stage. The government must take immediate action and take the necessary measures. Nothing is superior to human life. In this regard, we have 98 of our Constitution, 104 of our Civil Code, and 105. We demand that the matter be investigated by the establishment of the research commission. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly We propose and to follow the conditions of prisoners and prisoners whose health is worsening and in line with the purpose of their hunger strikes, to investigate what should be done for dialogue and negotiations, as the Grand National Assembly of Turkey sees the critical stage of hunger strikes in the prisons in Turkey. On 12 September 2012, 63 prisoners and prisoners in different prisons began an unchanging hunger strike with demands of "the end of isolation and isolation policies imposed on the native language of education, defence and Abdullah Ocalan." The number has reached 707, with hundreds of prisoners and prisoners on hunger strike with the same demands. As of today, the health of prisoners and prisoners on hunger strike that reached their 51st day is getting worse and worse. Hunger and inadequacy lead to severe body destruction, deaths, and particularly neurological patterns. Multiple arrests and convicts appear to be suffering from dizziness, fatigue, joints and headaches, disorderly hypotensive or elevated blood pressure, weight loss, drinking, insomnia, etc. It's coming. The hunger strike must have averaged 1.5-2 lt/day water and 2 gr/day salt purchases. The amount of sugar taken during the hunger strike must be at least 200-250 grams per day. But in order for the sugar to become the energy that the body needs, we need what we call vitamin B1, a molecule called tiamine. Vitamin B1 should be taken at least 500 milligrams per day if possible. Since there are no pure B1 preparates in our country, Neurovit, Benexol, Apikobal, and one of the equivalent preparats should be preferred and should be taken two tablets a day, if possible. Bemix draje and equivalent preparats with 10 milligrams of vitamin B1 should not be preferred. Hunger strikers need vitamin B1 to use their sugar. Taking vitamin B1 will therefore not disrupt the hunger strike, but will allow the hunger strike to reach its true goal because it allows the use of the reverse sugar. Since the hunger strike is not an act of suicide, it is imperative that the hunger strikers receive vitamin B1 in order to have physical and mental conditions to continue their lives. With vitamin B1 taking such a big deal, let's leave it in many prisons, where Neurovit, BenexoI, ApikobaI, and one of the common value preparats, the purchase of the B vitamins taken to the prisoners are not entered into prisons by the prison administration. Following the critical days of hunger strikes in prisons, Ankara's Medical Chamber, Modern Legalists' Office, Human Rights Association Ankara Branch, Health and Social Services Union (SES) Ankara Branch, Human Rights Foundation of Turkey (TİHV) and the Federation of Compounded Families Law and Solidarity Associations formed a monitoring committee. The monitoring team has also stated that serious steps must be taken to assess the demands of the government, prisoners and prisoners and to resolve the problem through dialogue and negotiation immediately. However, both the Prime Minister and other government officials appear to be far from the solution of the problem by dialogue and threatening to intervene. The result of a brutal operation under the name "return to life" on December 19, 2000 is well-known. The key thing to remember is that the hunger strike is not a suicide form, but an act of action. In this context, it is not for the conditions of hundreds of prisoners and prisoners in prisons, but for the immediate fulfillment of steps to resolve the Kurdish problem, which is the country's biggest problem and the bleeding wound. With action, every citizen seeking peace and negotiations in Turkey should hear the voice of hundreds of prisoners and prisoners on hunger strike and act on the Kurdish issue for negotiations and solutions. A parliamentary research commission must be set up immediately to monitor the process of prisons in Turkey, follow the conditions of prisoners and prisoners whose health is worsening, and to investigate what should be done for dialogue and negotiations, in line with the purpose of hunger strikes. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly "I would like to ask that a parliamentary investigation be opened under Articles 98 of the Constitution, Article 104 and 105 of the Civil Code in order to address the loss of rights and victims of citizens who have been deprived of defence in the main language and to investigate the resolution of this issue, which is already a demand for prisoners on the 52nd day of the hunger strike. 02.11.2012 "The language is usually the right of societies to the existence of individuals in private. No authority can interfere with this right in an arbitrary, illegal, unfair manner. The linguistic difference is an element and the future of the world's cultural heritage. All languages spoken around the world are vital. The importance referred to in this context is not the facilitation of international communication using multi-use languages, but the concern of providing different languages. As a matter of fact, the agreement of the establishment of the Republic of Turkey is the third of the Treaty of Lausanne. It contains provisions for the protection of cultural rights. While part of these provisions are aimed at protecting non-Muslim minorities in Turkey, some of their provisions guarantee the rights of different cultures, which are not in the status of non-Muslim minorities, including Kurds. Article 39 of the Lausanne Agreement's 4th statement: "No restrictions will be placed on the use of any Turkish national in private or trade relations, religion, press or all kinds of publications in open meetings." It's shaped like this. This joke gives all Turkish citizens the right to use any language they want anywhere and any time. Those who are entitled to this joke are all citizens, and the practical response is "the non - Turkish language of citizens. "Despite the official language of the state, Turkish nationals who speak a language other than Turkish will be made available to the courts in order to use their own language verbally," the 5th joke of the 39th Article of the Lausanne Agreement said. Unlike the rights of "Muslim minorities," in this joke, all non-Turkish citizens of the Republic of Turkey have been allowed to use their own language in court. The definition of "official language, although the state has an official language," has also indicated that "official language" should not interfere with the use of other languages. As a matter of fact, the meaning of "their own language" in the text of matter is the native language of the parties. No matter how well he understands the language of the trial/official language, or how well he speaks it, the language of the concerned can best express itself is the main language as a principle. The purpose of this verdict is to ensure that the right to defend is carried out in the best possible manner during the trial. However, in court trials, the demands of individuals to "defensive in the native language" have been rejected on various grounds, and the right to defend them has been ruled unlawfully. "If he insists on defending himself in another language, he will be considered entitled to silence." His sentence has been seen in our courts for some time now in the form of a decision or a court order. In fact, some courts have given one of the two defendants charged with the same crime a reduction of "good behavior in the trial" to one of the two defendants in Turkish, while refusing to give the second one the same discount just because he defended Kurdish. I mean, the law has been created, the rights of people are clearly condemned without any basis. The fact that the right hand of rights was done by the law is another aspect of the job. Defence rights in the main language are reflected in the hearing logs by definitions such as "unknown language" and "executive language." In Turkey's geography, where 36 different languages are spoken, there is also evidence of how the person's most natural rights have been ignored. Courts clearly violate the Lausanne Agreement. These positions of the courts are not only in violation of Lauzan 39/5, but also in violation of the Constitution's "Detainment of the Rights of Rights" in Article 36, titled "Freedom of Rights." While the right to defend the main language is a legal right, it has been ignored by the stance of political will and judiciary, while its use has been lifted to the illegal ground. It is important that Parliament investigates Article 104 and 105 of the 98th Civil Code of the Constitution, in order to ensure that this legal right is resolved in order not to be arbitrary. Mr. President, the high-profile lawmakers have been given the grounds for their questioning, allegations that our Labour Minister has not developed policies to prevent business accidents and has not taken measures. My dear friends, our Ministry of Labor is making great efforts to create a world-class framework and working environment in the field of occupational health and safety. As part of the development in the field of occupational health and safety, the Law on Business Health and Safety No. 6331 was adopted on June 30, 2012, in line with the Charter of the European Union's Charter and agreements 155 and 161 of the ILO. Thus, during the AK PARTI rule, occupational health and safety were first discussed by a separate law. 36 regulations and six notifications have been published in the Official Gazette, following the legislation of the Special Health and Safety Act, which includes all employees, based on the preventive approach and risk assessment. Following the release of the Law, our Ministry of Labor prioritized promotions and information in 81 provinces, held seminars, sent briefings to workplaces, as well as employment organisations, industrial and trade rooms, and a total of 272 information articles have been sent to organised industrial areas. In addition, ILO contracts 155 and 161 were approved in 2004 and ILO Convention 187 in 2013 in our country. The ILO Convention on Health and Safety was published in the Official Gazette in 2014. The legalisation process of ILO Convention No. 176 on Health and Security in the mines is under way in the Grand National Assembly of Turkey and has been adopted by the General Assembly. With our ministry's initiatives, Turkey has attended several international meetings on labour health and safety and has hosted some. There is no doubt that the biggest part in reducing business accidents and the destruction of occupational diseases is the development of business health and safety culture, the discovery of awareness. To this end, a total of 26 projects were carried out in Turkey, from 2002 to 2012, including 11 international projects, 15 national resources. In addition, there has been a significant increase in the number of occupational health and safety institutions and professionals since the publication of Law 6331. These professionals, at the same time, have started working in the field. By the ministry, 83,000 occupational safety experts, 23,000 workplace physicians have been assigned, 1,800 OSGB and 220 educational institutions have been authorized in this area. In addition, 200,000 occupational safety experts and workplace physicians signed agreements between their workplaces. All of these studies show that the Ministry is struggling to meet world standards for labour health and safety. I salute the General Assembly. The concepts of mandatory military, nation state and national sovereignty are fundamentally defining in international relations in the 19th and 20th centuries. The change across the world in the 1990s and the end of the Cold War, which marked the politics of European states after World War II, have also led to changes in terms of mandatory military service. Also, the war in Yugoslavia and NATO intervention in the 1990s have shown that national defence is no longer the primary military priority. So in recent years, some of the countries of the European Union have chosen to give up mandatory military service, while all have accepted it as a right to a conscientious rejection. Dear lawmakers, the importance of military service in Turkey referred to in the traditional frame is a problematic approach when the emphasis on the vitalness of national defence policy and, for example, the emphasis on the sanctity of military service, which is being taught in school textbooks, under the scope of modern-day universal human rights and civic freedoms. In Turkey, it is not a secret that thousands of citizens who have reached military age each year strive to delay this obligation as much as possible, while citizens whose economic situation is good are not hoping for alternative exemptions, such as the priced military. On the other hand, when international organisations for the adoption of conscientious objectiveism in all Western countries in particular in Europe have jointly assessed the trends in reorganisation of the Armed Forces of Turkey, debates have been raised in Turkey as well as behind the era of forced military and conscientious rejection and in light of global developments. Dear lawmakers, one of the things that causes serious victims on the axis of military debates is a matter of conscientious rejection. Conscientious rejection is one's refusal to become a soldier for religious, philosophical, political, moral reasons and to be taken under arms. Hence, there are various reasons why people are inclined to be conscientious objectors. A citizen may be a conscientious objector, of course, because he refuses to be put under arms because of his religious beliefs, philosophical, political, moral ideas, and because he is opposed to wars and armies that run them, or whether he wants to give them orders or not. Mr. President, high-value lawmakers are now guaranteed the right of citizens to conscientious rejection in all European Union countries by law. Again, near all of these countries, citizens are given the opportunity to serve alternative public services as alternatives to military service under arms. In these countries, conscientious objectors offer services in hospitals, civil society organisations, education and charities, immigration offices, rehabilitation centers, fire and rescue organisations and poets. Dear lawmakers, it is clear that democracy is restricted to individuals and communities in countries where there is mandatory, alternative and long-term military practice. In another way, the state-personal relationship, the state's obligation to military service for its own demands and interests, is a concept that restricts individual freedoms. Circumstances of mandatory military service have been transformed around the world, and this transformation is still happening. In connection with this, the necessity of mandatory military service has become a subject of debate. In many countries, military service is reorganised in terms of transformations in the armed forces. As some countries move to professional service, their military term in some countries is shortened, while the number of chartered soldiers in the armies has increased relatively. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, with the development of democracy and the rise of freedom demands, state-to-state and individual relations must also experience transformation. This transformation should tend to promote rights demands in favour of democracy and freedoms. An important phase of this trend should be the restructuring of the military institution. Of course, these fundamental arguments are being held in Turkey as well. Turkey's obligation to take steps on this issue, especially as our citizens have increased their right to conscientious objection and the international institutions have concentrated their demands on this issue. These demands and demands from the community and international institutions, as well as violations within the military, clearly reveal the distortion of the mandatory military system. Dear MPs, the violations of the rights of our young citizens who have served their troops during military service also expose another distortion of mandatory military practice. Military deaths, known in public as suspected soldier deaths in the military, should be focused on serious levels of depression and suicide in soldiers exposed to psychological pressure, as a result of the arbitrary practices of upper-level command. Dear lawmakers, an important problem caused by mandatory military service is the economic challenges of military citizens and their families during the military service. A citizen who quits his job and goes into the army, and if he is married, his family or those who are obliged to care for him, his income problems continue to create significant problems. A citizen under arms is carrying out a civic duty as defined by the law. However, when they are providing for their spouse, their children, or their parents, whom they are obliged to care for, when they are forced to serve, suddenly they are deprived of their income. From this point of view, it is clear that the state has not fulfilled its homework due to the social state principle against the individual who is obliged to do its civic duty. In terms of economics, analysis of military systems has shown that countries that use mandatory military service as a model are further behind by the level of production and economic growth rate compared to countries that have professional armies. Studies have shown that mandatory military service for OECD countries has a statistically meaningful negative effect on economic performance. For these reasons, mandatory military service is described as the most costly military model for the long term due to the negativeness of human and physical capital savings. Current data and opinions show that, with the increasing level of prosperity, especially in developed and developing countries, resistance and reluctance to enforce military service have increased, and the participation in armies is insufficient. The negative response and reluctance to mandatory military service is increasing in Turkey as well. This assessment is confirmed by the continued growth of the non-military population for various reasons, the organisational display of increased demands and expectations and responses for paid military service. The subject of the priced military service that we're talking about with the proposal of the law is a government cover-up and, by the way, an unfair attempt to collect money from citizens. In Turkey, the practice of paid military service has become a privilege granted to the children of the wealthy elite, at the risk of violating the principle of equality in the law. Dear MPs, Turkey must step up its development in accordance with universal principles such as democracy, freedoms, human rights. Alternative options for armed, mandatory and long-term military form should be developed, and regulations for the ability of our citizens to serve in different public service institutions should be made. In accordance with the concept of the professional military, where developed countries evolve, the contractual, paid military practice must be expanded. In the long run, mandatory military practice must be completely eliminated. The right to conscientious rejection of our citizens, who do not wish to serve for different reasons, must be recognized immediately.
